# ICEM design gradients: tables (issue #1177)

Generated by `analyze.py` from `runs/`. Units: N m per modelled sector (LENGTH included); gradients in N m per parameter unit (m, deg, 1).

## Condensed: worst case per parameter

Largest best-step FD relative error and narrowest Taylor window over both couplings, both designs and all current levels (zero current included where run), per mesh level and current rule. The case is `mode/design/current`. Two window measures: anchored at the reference step (the #1174 definition), and the widest factor-2 window over any anchor (which is not fooled by a sign change of the remainder at the reference step).

## Gradient vs Newton tolerance

Largest relative change of a gradient entry from its rel_tol = 1e-14 value, over all 12 parameters, per run and current.

scaled = |Δ dJ/dθ_k| · w_k / |J|, the change of the first-order objective change across parameter k's box width w_k (`reference_data.json` bounds; 40 deg for the angle, 1.5 for ScaleR), relative to the objective: a unit-free measure that is not inflated by a near-zero gradient entry.

## Adjoint gradient against mesh level

The adjoint gradient (rel_tol 1e-14) on the NURBS meshes L0 to L3 of the family (L3 from the gradient-only runs). d_ij = g(Lj) - g(Li), relative to |g| at the finest level; r = |d01/d12| and |d12/d23| (about 4 for O(h²) convergence, 2 for O(h)). scaled = |d of the finest pair| · w_k / |J|: the change of the first-order objective change across the box, relative to J. Full current.

## Moved-mesh gradient against remeshed central differences

h45 coupling torque, initial design. The adjoint is on a fresh mesh of theta0 built from the construction's curves (`build_design_mesh.py`); the moved FD re-moves that mesh, the remeshed FD meshes theta0 ± h afresh. gap = (remeshed FD - adjoint) / |adjoint|. residual = |J+ - J- - 2h dJ| / 2 (N m): what the difference cannot attribute to the adjoint (remeshing noise plus truncation).

### Trend with refinement

noise = median of |J+ - J- - 2h dJ| / 2 over h ≤ 1e-5 (N m): the jitter of the objective between fresh meshes of nearly the same geometry. rm-mv@h = (remeshed FD - moved FD) / |adjoint| at step h: both differences carry the same truncation, so this is the remeshing effect alone. noise/(h|dJ|) is the part of rm-mv@h the jitter alone can explain. adjoint fresh vs NURBS = relative difference of the adjoints on the two θ0 meshes of the level. rm-mv slope = the least-squares slope (through 0, over all 7 steps) of the per-step difference between the remeshed and the moved (J+ - J-)/2, over |dJ|: the systematic remeshed-vs-moved derivative gap with the jitter averaged (dominated by the largest steps).

## Cost

Median of the repeats, one process, sequential, same host. forward = one evaluation of the 10-angle sweep (zero start, then warm from the previous angle); gradient = forward + 10 adjoints + ∂X/∂θ + contractions for all 12 parameters and both objectives; FD = 2 × 12 forwards, each warm-started from the base states (h = 1e-6).

## Per-parameter validation (step sweeps that re-move the mesh)

Best central-difference relative error over the sweep (and its step), and the Taylor O(h²) window: the run of steps over which remainder/h² stays within a factor 2 of its value at the reference step (1e-5 m; 1e-2 deg for the angle; 1e-4 for ScaleR), as `h_hi..h_lo (decades)`. Steps are in metres, degrees (OPERATING_ANGLE) or 1 (ScaleR). LENGTH is linear and ScaleR (prescribed rule) an exact s², so their remainder is round-off (LENGTH) or exact (ScaleR) and their windows are not O(h²) tests.
